Council-Regular Meeting   A2        
Meeting Date: 01/06/2026  
To: Mayor and Council
Submitted For: Jason Angell, Development Services Director 
From: Matt Taylor, Planning Manager
Date: January 6, 2026
Strategic Plan Focus Area:
Not Applicable 
Subject: PUBLIC HEARING: Ordinance No. 2026.002: Relating to Development; approving a rezoning of
approximately 300.5 acres of land located west of Interstate 10 along North Luckett Road and just
south of the Pinal County line in a portion of Section 1, Township 11, Range 10E, from R-144
(Residential) to SP (Specific Plan), creating the Luckett Road North Specific Plan; and approving a
minor amendment to the General Plan amending the land use category from Employment to
Master Planned Area (Jason Angell)
Resolution No. 2026-005: Relating to Development; declaring as a public record filed with the
Town Clerk the Luckett Road North Specific Plan adopted by Ordinance No. 2026.002 (Jason
Angell)
Discussion:
Request
Lazarus and Silvyn, PC, representing the property owner, the Corporation of the Presiding Bishop of The Church
of Jesus Christ of Latter-Day Saints, is requesting approval of the Luckett Road North Specific Plan, to
rezone approximately 300.5 acres of land from R-144 (Residential) to SP (Specific Plan). The
proposed rezoning is accompanied by a request for a minor amendment to the Marana General Plan
Land Use category designation from Employment to Master Planned Area.   
Marana 2040 General Plan
The 2040 Marana General Plan designates the future land use of the area proposed to be rezoned as
Employment. A minor amendment to the General Plan accompanies this rezoning proposal to change
the designation to Master Planned Area. 
Surrounding Land Use and Zoning
The property located to the west has the same owner; it is currently farmed and is also used for solar
energy production and storage. The property is also being farmed to the south and a similar
rezoning has been proposed for a data center development in conjunction with this
application. Property to the north, which lies in Pinal County, is
home to an existing sand and gravel mining operation.  The Arizona Veteran's Memorial Cemetery is
located to the east; this site contains several structures.  Zoning to the north in Pinal County is
industrial, while land in all other directions has a rural residential zoning designation.
Proposed Land Use
The site is proposed to be used as a data center in conformance with section 17-6-13 of the Marana
Town Code. Data centers are specialized facilities designed to house computer systems, servers, and
related hardware that store, process, and manage vast amounts of digital information. Related
infrastructure improvements, including a substation, drainage improvements, and street and utility
extensions are also proposed.
Access and Circulation
The site will be accessed via two driveways on Luckett Road on the east side of the property.  A
proposed extension of Luckett Road to the north to Pinal Airpark Road will provide regional access and
a direct route to US I-10.
Utilities
The Marana Town Code requires estimates of future energy and water needs, as well as commitments
from utility providers.  A 2.25-mile waterline extension for potable water is proposed to be constructed
from the site to the intersection of Wentz and Sagebrush Road, where the existing waterline ends. 
Industrial, irrigation, and fire water supply will be withdrawn from the adjacent CMID irrigation canal.
A new 2-mile force sewer main is proposed to be constructed within the Luckett Road right-of-way
south to where Luckett intersects with Sagebrush Road.  A sewer lift station will also be required to
reach the existing sewer main at Sagebrush.
The project will need an estimated 550 to 750 megawatts of power at full build-out. The applicant is
currently engaged with Trico Electric to ensure a future energy supply.
Fire Service
The property is located within the Avra Valley Fire District.  A water storage tank and booster station will
be installed as part of this project.
Citizen Participation
A neighborhood meeting was held virtually via Microsoft Teams on November 19th at 6:00 PM; there
were approximately ten neighbors present.  An overview of the project was given.  The only question
that arose was regarding the expected opening date.  The applicant indicated that the anticipated
opening would be in either 2028 or 2029.
Public Notification
This public hearing was noticed in The Daily Territorial newspaper, and all property owners within 300
feet of the rezoning area were noticed by United States Mail of the date, time, and topic of the public
hearing.  Additional notice was posted at the property, various locations within the community and on
the Town's website. 
Case Analysis Review Criteria
The applicant has satisfied the requirements of section 17-4-15 of the Town Code, which outlines the
necessary contents of a specific plan.  The applicant has also addressed the requirements for data
centers found in section 17-6-13(B) of the Town Code, including the provision of a baseline noise study,
identification of the intended sources of water and electric, and an assessment of future water and energy needs.
Pursuant to Town Code section 17-3-1(D), the Planning Commission and Town Council shall also
consider the following questions, at a minimum, in reviewing an application for rezoning:
1. Any change of character in the area due to installation of public facilities, other zone changes, new
growth trends, deterioration, and development;
Based upon the requirements in the Town Code and the proposed conditions, the rezoning amendment does not appear to be
detrimental to the character of the area due to installation of public facilities or any changes associated with the rezoning.
2. The degree to which the proposed zoning will benefit the community; whether there will be benefits
derived by the community, or area, by granting the proposed rezoning;
Based upon the requirements of the Town Code and the recommended conditions, this rezoning will benefit the community by
providing the extension of streets, utilities, and drainage infrastructure.
3. Whether the proposed rezoning is compatible with the surrounding area or whether there will be
adverse impacts on the capacity or safety of the portion of street network influenced by the rezoning,
parking problems, or environmental impacts that the new use will generate such as excessive
storm runoff, water, air, or noise pollution, excessive nighttime lighting, or other nuisances;
Based upon the requirements in the Town Code and the recommended conditions, the proposed rezoning is compatible with
the surrounding area, and any potential impacts will be mitigated with the extension of public infrastructure and noise
attenuation.
4. Whether the proposal conforms with and is in furtherance of the implementation of the goals and
policies of the general plan, other adopted plans, and the goals, objectives and policies of the land
development code, and other town regulations and guidelines, including goals and policies relating to
economic development;
With the acceptance of the recommended conditions and the approval of the accompanying minor General Plan Amendment
from Employment to Master Planned Area, the rezoning amendment conforms with the goals and policies of the general plan,
the land development code, and other town regulations and guidelines. 
5. The zoning districts and existing land uses of the surrounding properties;
Considering its location, and based upon the requirements of the Town Code and recommended conditions, the proposed
rezoning is compatible with the existing zoning and uses of surrounding properties .
6. Whether the existing and proposed transportation infrastructure is suitable and adequate to serve the
traffic anticipated to be generated by the proposed development:
With the acceptance of the recommended conditions and considering the proposed infrastructure improvements, the existing
and proposed transportation infrastructure is suitable and adequate to serve the traffic anticipated to be generated by the
proposed development. 
7. Whether the existing and proposed utility infrastructure, public facilities, and public services are
suitable and adequate to serve the proposed rezoning area;
Based upon the requirements of the Town Code and recommended conditions, and considering the proposed extension of
public infrastructure, there is adequate utility infrastructure, public facilities, and public services in the area to serve the
proposed rezoning area. 
8. Whether the subject property is suitable for the uses to which it has been restricted under the existing
zoning classification:
Considering the location, and based upon the requirements of the Town Code and recommended conditions, the subject
property is suitable for the proposed uses. 
9. Whether the rezoning is compatible with the adjacent neighborhood, especially residential
neighborhood stability and character:
Based upon the rezoning requirements and conditions and considering the location, this rezoning amendment is compatible
with the adjacent neighborhood in area stability and character.
10. If applicable, the length of time the subject property has remained vacant as zoned:
The parcel has remained vacant since its annexation by the Town in 2001.
11. Whether there is an adequate supply of land available in the subject area and the surrounding
community to accommodate the zoning and community needs:
Considering its location, there is an adequate supply of land available in the area and the surrounding community to
accommodate the zoning and community needs. 
Staff Recommendation:
This is a discretionary item for the Town Council to consider. Should the Town Council choose to approve this
item, staff recommends it be based upon the recommended findings and subject to the conditions set forth in the
draft ordinance attached. 
Planning Commission Recommendation
The proposed rezoning was considered at a public hearing before the Marana Planning Commission on December
10, 2025.  The Planning Commission voted unanimously (6-0 with one commissioner absent) to recommend to
Town Council approval of the requested rezoning.
Suggested Motion:
I move to approve Ordinance No. 2026.002, approving a rezoning of approximately 300.5 acres of land located
west of Interstate 10 along North Luckett Road and just south of the Pinal County line from R-144 (Residential) to
SP (Specific Plan), creating the Luckett Road North Specific Plan; and approving a minor amendment to the
General Plan amending the land use category from Employment to Master Planned Area; and Resolution No.
2026-005, declaring as a public record filed with the Town Clerk the Luckett Road North Specific Plan adopted by
Ordinance No. 2026.002.

Ordinance No. 2026.002 - 1 -
MARANA ORDINANCE NO. 2026.002 
RELATING TO DEVELOPMENT; APPROVING A REZONING OF APPROXIMATELY 
300.5 ACRES  OF  LAND LOCATED  WEST  OF  INTERSTATE  10 ALONG  NORTH 
LUCKETT ROAD AND JUST SOUTH OF THE PINAL COUNTY LINE IN A PORTION 
OF SECTION 1, TOWNSHIP  11, RANGE  10E, FROM  R-144 (RESIDENTIAL) TO SP 
(SPECIFIC PLAN), CREATING THE LUCKETT ROAD NORTH SPECIFIC PLAN; AND 
APPROVING A MINOR AMENDMENT TO THE GENERAL PLAN AMENDING THE 
LAND USE CATEGORY FROM EMPLOYMENT TO MASTER PLANNED AREA 
WHEREAS the Corporation of the Presiding Bishop of The Church of Jesus Christ 
of Latter-Day Saints (collectively the “Property Owners”) own 300.5 acres of land located 
west of Interstate 10 along North Luckett Road and just south of the Pinal County line in 
Section 1, Township 11 South, Range 10 East, described on Exhibit “A” attached to and 
incorporated in this ordinance by this reference (the “Rezoning Area”); and 
WHEREAS the Property Owners have authorized Lazarus & Silvyn, P.C. to submit 
an application to rezone the Rezoning Area from R-144 (Residential) to SP (Specific Plan) 
(“this Rezoning”) and to amend the Make Marana 2040 General Plan (the “General Plan”) 
land use category from Employment to Master Planned Area; and 
WHEREAS the Marana Planning Commission held a public hearing to consider 
this Rezoning on December 10, 2025, and voted 6 to 0 with one Commissioner absent to 
recommend that the Town Council approve this Rezoning, subject to the recommended 
conditions; and 
WHEREAS the Mara na Town Counc il held a public hearing to consider this 
Rezoning on January 6, 2026, and determined that this Rezoning should be approved. 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT ORDAINED by the Mayor and Council of the Town of 
Marana, Arizona, as follows:  
Section 1.  The zoning of  the  Rezoning Area is  hereby  changed  from  R-144 
(Residential) to SP (Specific Plan), creating the  Luckett Road  North Specific Plan.  The 
Luckett Road North Specific Plan, one  electronic and one printed copy of which are on 
file in the office of the Town Clerk of the Town of Marana, Arizona, which was made a 
public record by and is attached as Exhibit A to Marana Resolution No. 2026-005, is 
hereby referred to, adopted and made part of this ordinance as if fully set out here. 
Ordinance No. 2026.002 - 2 -
Section 2. A minor amendment to the General Plan is hereby approved, changing 
the General Plan designation of the Rezoning Area from Employment to Master Planned 
Area. 
Section 3. This Rezoning is subject to the following conditions, the violation of 
which shall be treated in the same manner as a violation of the Town of Marana Land 
Development Code (but which shall not cause a reversion of this Rezoning), and which 
shall be binding  on the Property Owners,  the applicant, and any other relevant parties 
involved in this particular rezoning  and their successors in interest (all of whom are 
collectively included in the term “Property Owners” in the following conditions): 
1. Compliance with all applicable provisions of the Town’s codes and ordinances
current at the time of any subsequent development including, but not limited to,
requirements for public improvements  and payment of application fees and
applicable development impact fees.
2. Any development plan for any portion of the Rezoning Area  shall be in general
conformance with the conceptual land use  plan presented to and approved by the
Town Council as part of this Rezoning.
3. A master drainage study must be submitted by the Property Owners and accepted by
the Town Engineer prior to Town approval of a development plan for any portion of
the Rezoning Area.
4. A detailed traffic impact analysis  (TIA) must be submitted by the Property Owner s
and accepted by Town staff prior to approval of a development plan  for any portion
of the Rezoning Area . The Property Owners may be required to submit an updated
TIA or updated traffic studies when warranted by changes to the Rezoning Area or in
development surrounding the Rezoning Area.
5. A water infrastructure and phasing plan (WIP)  must be submitted by the Property
Owners and accepted by the Marana Water Department (the “water provider”) prior
to approval of a development plan for any portion of the Rezoning Area. The WIP
shall identify all on -site and off -site water facilities needed to serve  the proposed
development. The WIP shall include all information required by the water provider,
such as (but not limited to) analysis of water use and fire flow requirements, and well
source, reservo ir, and booster station infrastructure  needed to serve the proposed
development. If the water provider requires a water service agreement as a condition
of service to the proposed development, the Property Owners must enter into a water
service agreement with the water provider consistent with the accepted WIP. The
Property Owners may be required to submit an updated WIP when warranted by
changes to the Rezoning Area or in development surrounding the Rezoning Area.
6. A master sewer plan must be submitted by the Property Owners and accepted by the
Marana Water Department (the “ wastewater u tility”) prior to the approval of any
development plan for the Rezoning Area. The master sewer plan shall identify all on-
site and off-site wastewater facilities needed to serve the proposed development, and
Ordinance No. 2026.002 - 3 -
shall include all information required by the wastewater utility . If the wastewater 
utility requires a sewer service agreement as a condition of service to the proposed 
development, the Property Owners must enter into a sewer service agreement with 
the wastewater utility consistent with the accepted master sewer plan . The Property 
Owners may be required to submit an updated sewer plan when warranted by 
changes to the Rezoning Area or in development surrounding the Rezoning Area. 
7. The Property Owners must design and construct any roadway, drainage, water, and
wastewater improvements, and dedicate or acquire any property rights  associated
with those improvements, that the Town requires based on the data and findings of
the accepted traffic impact analysis, the accepted master drainage study, the accepted
WIP, the accepted master sewer plan, and other studies approved in connection with
the approval of a preliminary plat (if applicable), final plat, or development plan for
any portion of the Rezoning Area.
8. The Property Owners shall install a non -potable water system for industrial and fire
suppression uses, and to serve the common open space areas and other landscaped
amenities, as accepted by the Cortaro-Marana Irrigation District.
9. No approval, permit or authorization by the Town of Marana authorizes violation of
any federal or state law or regulation or relieves the Property Owners from
responsibility to ensure compliance with all applicable federal and state laws and
regulations, including the Endangered Species Act and the Clean Water Act. The
Property Owners should retain appropriate experts and consult appropriate federal
and state agencies to determine any action necessary to assure compliance with
applicable laws and regulations.
10. The Property Owners shall not cause any lot split of any kind without the written
consent of the Town of Marana.
11. The Property Owners are responsible for paying the full costs of design and
construction of Luckett Road to a two-lane rural collector, per Marana Standard Detail
110-1, from the southern boundary of the Rezoning Area north to Pinal Airpark Road
(the “Luck ett Road Extension”). The Property Owners’ financial obligations under
this condition include paying the full costs for the acquisition of any property rights
associated with the improvements. The Town of Marana shall acquire any necessary
property rights for the Luckett Road Extension from the Arizona State Land
Department (ASLD), subject to full reimbursement by the Property Owners.
a. Within 30 days after the issuance of any grading permit in the Rezoning Area,
the Property Owners shall pay in cash to the Town a deposit in the full
estimated acquisition amount for the acquisition of property rights from ASLD.
b. Within 30 days after the issuance of any grading permit in the Rezoning Area
and the Town providing Property Owner s the full estimated costs of pre -
construction and design services for the Luckett Road Extension, the Property
Ordinance No. 2026.002 - 4 -
Owners shall pay in cash to the Town the full estimated cost  of the pre -
construction and design services.   
c. The Property Owners may participate in and provide input into the design of
the Luckett Road Extension.
d. Within 30 days after the Property Owners’ receipt of a written engineer’s
estimate based on 100% design plans from the Town, the Property Owners
shall pay in cash to the Town the full estimated cost of construction of the
Luckett Road Extension.  Property Owners shall be responsible only for actual
construction costs by the contractor with no additional Town administrative or
other fees.    In lieu of providing payment to the Town for construction costs as
described in this condition, the Property Owners may elect to construct the
Luckett Road Extension, pursuant to the design procured by the Town, by
providing the Town with written notice of  their election within 30 days after
the Town’s delivery of the engineer’s estimate to the Property Owners.
e. No later than 60 days after final completion of the Luckett Road Extension, the
Town shall provide to the Property Owner s an itemized statement of the
Town’s actual costs for the Luckett Road Extension  and a check or invoice
representing the difference between the Town’s costs and the Property
Owners’ payments to the Town. If the Property Owners’ payments to the Town
were insufficient to cover the Property Owners’  obligations pursuant to this
ordinance, the Property Owners shall pay the Town the balance of the costs as
set forth in the invoice within 30 days after the itemized statement and invoice
are provided to the Property Owners. Any amounts unpaid after 30 days shall
accrue simple interest at the rate of 10% per year until paid in full
f. The completion of the Luckett Road Extension is not a prerequisite for the
Town to issue certificates of occupancy for structures in the Rezoning Area.
12. The Property Owners shall coordinate with the Town to manage construction traffic
for the Rezoning Area consistent with the provisions of the TIA.
13. The Property Owners shall construct an 8 -inch water main from the Rezoning Area
southerly along Luckett Road , easterly along  Hardin Road, southerly along  Wentz
Road, to the intersection of Wentz Road and Sagebrush Road. The final alignment will
be determined by Town water modeling.
14. The Property Owners shall construct a new sewer force main align ing with the
Luckett Road public right-of-way from the Rezoning Area  southerly to a point of
connection to the exis ting 27-inch gravity sewer at  the intersection with Sagebrush
Road.
15. The Property Owners shall construct a  new sewer lift station to serve the Rezoning
Area. Final location  of the sewer lift station will be confirmed through the design
process.
Ordinance No. 2026.002 - 5 -
16. The Property Owners shall construct the portion of a drainage channel referred to in
the Town of Marana Northwest Area Drainage Master Plan as the “10.5 Channel” that
is located within the Rezoning Area  (except as necessary to match current , offsite,
downstream flow patterns ) in conjunction with the owners of the property
immediately to the south, identified as Assessor’s Parcel Number (APN) 208-09-001E.
The Property Owners shall design the entire 10.5 Channel, including the portions of
the channel located outside the boundaries of the Rezoning Area, to 30% plans, which
must be approved by the Town, and construct the portion of the 10.5 Channel that is
in the Rezoning Area to those 30% plans. That constructed portion of the 10.5 Channel
will have no outfall until construction of the 10.5 Channel is fully completed (by the
Town). Until that occurs , the excavated channel segment in the Rezoning Area will
serve as detention/retention for the onsite development. After offsite construction of
the 10.5 Channel is complete, the onsite retention requirements will be accomplished
via recharge within the 10.5 Channel and/or the Santa Cruz River.   The Property
Owners shall assign to the Town any and all of the Property Owners’ rights to the
30% design plans . If APN 208-09-001E is not developed in conjunction with the
Rezoning Area, the Property Owners shall not be required to construct the 10.5
Channel, but will still be required to design the 10.5 Channel to 30% plans , and shall
construct the drainage infrastructure required to serve the Rezoning Area as
determined by the master drainage study.  Upon demand of the Town, the Property
Owners shall dedicate to the Town at no cost the portion of the Rezoning Area where
the 10.5 Channel  is located or will be located , whether the cha nnel has been
constructed or not .  At the time of the dedication, the Property Owners shall
contribute their proportionate share of costs to convert the retention/detention
system in the Rezoning Area to a conveyance system.
17. Within 60 days after the adoption of this ordinance, the Property Owner s shall
provide the Development Services  Department with one electronic copy in PDF
format which will also include graphics of the conceptual land use  plan in JPEG or
other suitable format of the Luckett Road North Data Center Specific Plan as amended
by this ordinance.
18. This Rezoning is valid for five years from the date of Town Council approval; if the
Property Owners fail to have a development plan approved prior to the  end of the
five-year time period,  the Town may schedule a public hearing before the Town
Council to take administrative action to extend, remove , or determine compliance
with the schedule for development or legislative action to revert the property to the
previous zoning (R-144).
Section 4. All ordinances, resolutions and motions and parts of ordinances, 
resolutions, and motions of the Marana Town Council in conflict with the provisions of 
this ordinance are hereby repealed, effective as of the effective date of this ordinance. 
Section 5. If any section, subsection, sentence, clause, phrase or portion of this 
ordinance is for any reason held to be invalid or unconstitutional by the decision of any 
Ordinance No. 2026.002 - 6 -
court of competent jurisdiction, such decision shall not affect the validity of the remaining 
portions of this ordinance.

A. Background
Data centers are specialized faciliƟes
designed to house computer systems, servers
and related hardware that store, process and
manage vast amounts of digital informaƟon.
Data centers are the backbone of U.S.
technological advancement, driving
economic growth and securing naƟonal
le
adership in innovaƟon.  Through cloud
compuƟng, they support online educaƟon,
empower communiƟes to advance seamless
connecƟvity, and host AI systems that drive
personalized experiences, real-Ɵme
navigaƟon and criƟcal decision-making tools.
Demand for data centers is supported by a
diverse set of industries with durable long-
term employment and posiƟve ﬁscal impacts
for local communiƟes.
Long-term infrastructure investment
supports communiƟes within Pima County
(
the “County”), and speciﬁcally the Town of
Marana (the “Town”), to play a leading role
in an increasingly dynamic and
interconnected 21st century economy.
Approximately 300 acres (Parcel No. 208-08-
004A, the “Property”) located west of
Interstate 10 (“I-10”) along N. LuckeƩ Road
and just south of the Pinal County line, has
been idenƟﬁed as an ideal site for this type
of investment.  (See Exhibit I.A: Regional
Context.)
E
xhibit I. A: Regional Context
Exhibit A to Marana Resolution 2026-005

B. Project Overview
Fremont Peak ProperƟes, LLC (the “Applicant”)
is interested in developing the Property with a
d
ata center and related infrastructure
improvements (the “Project”) that are already
in
 high demand in northern Marana.  The
Project, enƟtled through the LuckeƩ Road
North Speciﬁc Plan (“Speciﬁc Plan”), posiƟons
the Town to become a hub for technology,
innovaƟon and business growth.  (See Exhibit
I.B.1: Speciﬁc Plan Boundaries.)
The Project will create high-quality jobs 
related to the InformaƟon Technology sector, 
which has been targeted as one of four key 
industries for employment growth within the 
Town.   
This Speciﬁc Plan has been draŌed in 
conformance with Marana Town Code 
(“MTC”) § 17-6-13, Data Centers.  PotenƟal 
impacts to adjacent uses will be minimized 
through strategic miƟgaƟon measures, as 
outlined in SecƟon IV, Development & Design 
RegulaƟons, such as: 
 limiƟng building heights to diminish
visual impacts;
 incorporaƟng adequate drainage
infrastructure to oﬀset an increase in
impervious surfaces; and
 providing ample setbacks and
buﬀeryards for sound aƩenuaƟon.

C. General Plan Amendment 
The Property is located in the General Plan’s North 
Growth Area, which is “envisioned to become a premier 
employment hub in Marana, concentrated around Pinal 
Airpark [that] will aƩract manufacturing, distribuƟon, 
and logisƟcs industries for its advantageous locaƟon 
between Pinal Airpark, Interstate 10, and the Union 
Paciﬁc Railroad, as well as the future Interstate 11 
corridor.”  As shown on Exhibit I.B.2, the General Plan 
Future Land Use Map designates the Property as 
Employment, which supports a range of industrial, 
warehousing, manufacturing, business parks and other 
uses that present employment opportuniƟes.  The 
Project will result in signiﬁcant infrastructure 
investment in North Marana, not only funding this 
Project, but also potenƟally serving as a catalyst for 
addiƟonal economic development in this area.   
Concurrent with this Speciﬁc Plan proposal is a request 
to amend the Property’s land use designaƟon from 
Employment to Master Planned Area (“MPA”).  Make 
Marana 2040’s Table 1-1 indicates a change from 
Employment to MPA qualiﬁes as a minor amendment.  
The MPA designaƟon “denotes areas that are guided by 
separate development approvals.  These approvals 
establish the land uses, densiƟes and intensiƟes of 
development, and the character of the area.”  MPA is 
meant for properƟes appropriately planned and 
designed within the context of its environment.  This 
Speciﬁc Plan is consistent with the intent of the MPA 
designaƟon and the vision for the Town’s North Growth 
Area as a future employment center, as well as the 
goals and policies of Make Marana 2040.

properƟes to the south and west are 
currently farmed and have no exisƟng 
onsite structures.  There is a County-

energy producƟon and baƩery storage use

sand and gravel mining operaƟon within 
Pinal County.  Adjacent to the Property’s 
eastern boundary is the Arizona Veteran’s 
Memorial Cemetery, which includes three 
single-story structures.

B. Topography 
1. Pre-Development Cross-Slope 
The majority of the Property is relaƟvely 
ﬂat with gradual sloping from east to west 
and approximately 18 feet of fall across the 
Property.  As shown on Exhibit II.B, the 
west porƟon of the Property is generally 
16 to 18 feet lower in elevaƟon than the 
east porƟon.  The Property contains some 
areas with slopes 15% or greater near the 
irrigaƟon canal and diversion channel at 
the northeast corner.   
 
2. Significant Topographic Features 
There are no hillside conservaƟon areas, 
rock outcrops, or signiﬁcant topographic 
features on the Property.  Areas around 
the northeast corner of the Property are 
built-up into earthen berms for ﬂood 
miƟgaƟon purposes.

C. Hydrology 
The Property and surrounding area are subject to runoﬀ originaƟng in the 
Tortolita Mountains to the east, which is conveyed in bridges and culverts 
through the Central Arizona Project (“CAP”) Canal and the Interstate 10/Union 
Paciﬁc Railroad (“UPRR”) transportaƟon corridor .  West of Interstate 10, runoﬀ 
ﬂows overland primarily in a sheet ﬂow manner, to the north and west toward 
the Santa Cruz River .  Runoﬀ impacƟng the Property is deﬁned by the Tortolita 
Alluvial Fan Study (“TAFS”), which is the basis for the eﬀecƟve Federal 
Emergency Management AdministraƟon (“FEMA”) Flood Zone designaƟons.  
The total drainage area within the TAFS models (HEC-1 and FLO-2D) is 174.9 
square miles.  Exhibit II.C.1 depicts the Property within the TAFS study area.  
Signiﬁcant alteraƟons have been made to the terrain in the vicinity due to 
historic agricultural acƟvity.  A higher resoluƟon HEC-RAS 2D model was 
created to beƩer model ﬂow paƩerns in the project vicinity.  The RAS 2D 
model uƟlizes inﬂow hydrographs generated by the eﬀecƟve TAFS model.   
Runoﬀ impacts the Property as sheet ﬂow across farm ﬁelds separated by 
graded roads and irrigaƟon canals.  In addiƟon, a diversion channel conveys 
runoﬀ across the northeast corner of the Property and to the west along the 
north Property line.  Exhibit II.C.2 depicts the diversion channel along with 
drainage paƩerns across the Property and the 100-year peak discharges at key 
cross secƟons.  The majority of the Property is characterized by shallow sheet 
ﬂooding.  The 100-year sheet ﬂood depths and peak discharges are depicted 
on Exhibit II.C.2.   
The Property is almost completely inundated by the 100-year ﬂood, and the 
majority of the Property lies within designated FEMA ﬂoodplains.  Most of the 
Property lies within Zone AO1, with small areas of Zone AO2 and Zone X 
(shaded) to the southeast.  The northeast corner is outside the FEMA 
ﬂoodplain (Zone X).  (See Exhibit II.C.2.)  Runoﬀ exits the Property to the west 
in a sheet ﬂow manner, with depths typically ranging between 1 and 2 feet.  
Flow within the diversion channel is conveyed westerly in an irregular oﬀsite 
channel along the northern Property line.

E. Soils & Geology 
The primary use of the Property is agricultural with irrigaƟon channels and 
berms around the perimeter and oﬀsite stormwater impacƟng the eastern 
Property line.  A preliminary geotechnical engineering ﬁeld exploraƟon was 
conducted, and preliminary borings generally encountered soŌ to medium, 
sƟﬀ, ﬁne-grained soils and loose sand soils with variable amounts of silt, clay 
and gravel to depths of approximately 19 to 28 feet below the exisƟng ground 
surface.  These upper soils were generally overlying very sƟﬀ to hard clay soils 
and dense to very dense sand soils.  Groundwater was not encountered in any 
test boring at the Ɵme of drilling.

A. Land Use 
Data centers are specialized faciliƟes designed to house computer systems, servers 
and related hardware that store, process and manage vast amounts of digital 
informaƟon.  Data centers are the backbone of U.S. technological advancement, 
driving economic growth and securing naƟonal leadership in innovaƟon.  Through 
cloud compuƟng, they support online educaƟon, empower communiƟes to advance 
seamless connecƟvity, and host systems that drive personalized experiences, real-Ɵme 
navigaƟon and criƟcal decision-making tools.  Demand for data centers is supported by 
a diverse set of industries with durable long-term employment and posiƟve ﬁscal 
impacts for local communiƟes.   
Long-term infrastructure investment supports communiƟes within the County, and 
speciﬁcally the Town, to play a leading role in an increasingly dynamic and 
interconnected 21st century economy.  The Property has been idenƟﬁed as an ideal 
site for this type of investment.   
The Applicant is interested in developing the Property with a data center and related 
infrastructure improvements that are already in high demand in northern Marana.  
The Speciﬁc Plan posiƟons the Town to become a hub for technology, innovaƟon and 
business growth.   
The Project will create high-quality jobs in the InformaƟon Technology sector, which 
has been targeted as one of four key industries for employment growth within the 
Town.   
This Speciﬁc Plan has been draŌed in conformance with MTC § 17-6-13, Data Centers.  
PotenƟal impacts to adjacent uses will be minimized through strategic miƟgaƟon 
measures, as outlined in SecƟon IV, Development & Design RegulaƟons, such as: 
 limiƟng building heights to diminish visual impacts; 
 incorporaƟng adequate drainage infrastructure to oﬀset an increase 
in impervious surfaces; and 
 providing ample setbacks and buﬀeryards for sound aƩenuaƟon. 
 
The Property is located in the General Plan’s North Growth Area, which is “envisioned to 
become a premier employment hub in Marana, concentrated around Pinal Airpark [that] will 
aƩract manufacturing, distribuƟon, and logisƟcs industries for its advantageous locaƟon 
between Pinal Airpark, Interstate 10, and the Union Paciﬁc Railroad, as well as the future 
Interstate 11 corridor.”  The General Plan Future Land Use Map designates the Property as 
Employment, which supports a range of industrial, warehousing, manufacturing, business 
parks and other uses that present employment opportuniƟes.  The Project will result in 
signiﬁcant infrastructure investment in North Marana, not only funding this Project, but also 
potenƟally serving as a catalyst for addiƟonal economic development in this area.   
A Conceptual Land Use Plan is provided as Exhibit III.A to illustrate how the Project may be 
developed.

B. Topography & Grading 
The proposed grading design will be largely inﬂuenced by oﬀsite drainage 
conveyance through and around the Property, as well as the minimum 
required ﬁnished ﬂoor elevaƟon building pads.  The building Finished Floor 
ElevaƟons (“FFE”)  will be constructed in accordance with FEMA and Town 
requirements as follows: in proposed CLOMR/LOMR ﬂood zone mapping Zone 
Shaded X, A or AE, a minimum of 1 foot above the 100-year WSEL; Zone AO-1, 
2 feet above highest adjacent natural grade (“HANG”); Zone AO-2, 3 feet 
above HANG; AO-3, 4 feet above HANG.  Areas not impacted by a mapped 
FEMA Zone (Unshaded Zone X) will have no minimum FFE requirement or a 
minimum of 2 feet above the highest adjacent local 100-year WSEL.  The ﬁll 
pads are proposed to be generally sloping at a 1% grade to the internal 
drainage basins.

driveways at two locaƟons along LuckeƩ Road on the east side of the Property.  
Each driveway is assumed to have one ingress lane and one egress lane.  Internal 
access lanes will be constructed per Town standards.  
Regional access to the Property will be via Pinal Airpark Road from the north by 
extending LuckeƩ Road into Pinal County.  Exhibit III.C illustrates two opƟons for 
the LuckeƩ Road extension.

D. Hydrology 
The Conceptual Land Use Plan (Exhibit III.A) includes two clusters of large, light 
industrial buildings with interconnecƟng road access, along with a single large uƟlity 
yard.  Exhibit III.D.1 depicts the drainage system under post-development condiƟons.  
All three development areas will be elevated above the 100-year ﬂood, consƟtuƟng 
signiﬁcant ﬂow obstrucƟons.  Oﬀsite runoﬀ will be captured in below-grade earthen 
perimeter channels and a single upstream detenƟon basin and conveyed along the 
north and south sides of the Property into a single, large spreader basin.  Culverts will 
be placed at two locaƟons (East and West) to maintain ﬂow connecƟvity across 
elevated access roads.  The constructed channels conveying higher ﬂow 
concentraƟons are designed to ﬂow at velociƟes below 3 feet per second but may 
require scour protecƟon at speciﬁc transiƟon points to alleviate erosion potenƟal.  
(See Exhibit III.D.2: Channel Cross-SecƟons.)  The perimeter drainage concept is 
designed to release runoﬀ as shallow sheet ﬂow at the same locaƟons as exisƟng 
condiƟons, consistent with the Town of Marana Drainage Manual (“Drainage 
Manual”).  The downstream spreader basin is sized to release runoﬀ to the west over 
a low-level containment weir without increasing downstream 100-year water surface 
elevaƟons more than 0.1 feet relaƟve to exisƟng condiƟons.  The intent is to have no 
adverse impact with regards to ﬂow depths or erosion hazards to the exisƟng 
downstream ﬂoodplain areas.  This design approach is consistent with the Town’s 
development requirements and will serve as the basis for eventual Federal Emergency 
Management Agency (“FEMA”) CondiƟonal LeƩer of Map Revision (“CLOMR”) and 
LeƩer of Map Revision (“LOMR”) applicaƟons to remove the ﬂood zone designaƟons 
within the Property.  FEMA CLOMR/LOMR map revision applicaƟons will be based on 
eﬀecƟve TAFS FLO-2D hydrology, based on non-regional rainfall, in accordance with 
SecƟon 4.1 of the Drainage Manual.  It is anƟcipated that the map revision 
applicaƟons will uƟlize two dimensional HEC-RAS soŌware for updated hydraulic 
modeling of the post-development ﬂoodplain, in accordance with the Drainage 
Manual. 
Onsite runoﬀ increases due to substanƟally increased impervious areas will be 
miƟgated in accordance with the Drainage Manual with the installaƟon of mulƟple 
interior retenƟon basins, which will be sized to capture the total onsite ﬂow volume 
from successive 100-year and 10-year rainfall events.  All impervious development 
areas will drain into the interior basins.  Each basin will include low-ﬂow outlets to 
allow the basins to gradually drain into the perimeter drainage channels.  The 
downstream spreader basin and all four interior basins will require drywells to allow 
the basins to fully drain within the maximum storage Ɵme allowed. 
An alternaƟve drainage miƟgaƟon method has been suggested by the Town, which 
corresponds to Alternate 4B of the 2020 Marana Northwest Area Drainage Master 
Plan (“ADMP”).  Under this approach, a porƟon of the CMID 10.5 channel would be 
constructed within new public ROW along the south Property line and extending 
downstream the full distance to the Santa Cruz River ouƞall.  Design approval and 
construcƟon of the CMID 10.5 channel will require CLOMR and LOMR applicaƟons to 
the Town and FEMA.  The preliminary design capacity of the CMID 10.5 channel is 
1,654 cfs, which represents approximately half of the oﬀsite 100-year peak storm 
event reaching the Property under exisƟng condiƟons.  The applicant will work with 
the Town to design the CMID 10.5 channel as a water recharge structure, if feasible.  
With the addiƟon of the CMID 10.5 channel, the onsite perimeter channels, basins 
and culverts detailed in Exhibit III.D.1 could be substanƟally reduced and/or parƟally 
eliminated based on revised hydraulic modeling.  Under this opƟon, the onsite 
retenƟon requirements would be accomplished via direct discharge to the Santa Cruz 
River, while onsite detenƟon would be accomplished within the perimeter basins.  If 
other ADMP alternaƟve channels located farther upstream (along BarneƩ Road, 
Marana Road or Hardin Road) are fully implemented prior to development, there 
could be addiƟonal reducƟons to the oﬀsite runoﬀ reaching the Property.  As such 
improvements are deemed unlikely in the short term, these potenƟal future ﬂow 
reducƟons will have no impact on the current Project requirements.

E. Vegetation & Wildlife 
The proposed LuckeƩ Road extension will impact the Xeroriparian A riparian habitat 
located at the northeast corner of the Property.  The riparian vegetaƟon will be 
miƟgated through transplanƟng or replacing with planƟngs chosen to match adjacent 
undisturbed natural areas.  AddiƟonally, located primarily in the riparian area, species 
of naƟve plants, as listed on the Marana Protected NaƟve Plant List, will be impacted 
by the LuckeƩ Road extension.  The most notable of these species is the Prosopis, 
Parkinsonia and Acacia.  The naƟve plant preservaƟon plan will detail the miƟgaƟon 
approach for impacted vegetaƟon.  The balance of the site, used for the proposed 
data centers, is currently agricultural and does not contain riparian areas or protected 
naƟve plant species.  
The yellow-billed cuckoo, western burrowing owl and southwestern willow ﬂycatcher 
are idenƟﬁed within 3 miles of, and may inhabit, the Property.  The developer and 
contractor have addiƟonal responsibiliƟes prior to and during construcƟon to miƟgate 
the inﬂuence on these species: 
 The developer will employ a biologist to complete a pre-construcƟon survey for 
burrowing owls prior to construcƟon in all suitable habitats that will be 
disturbed.  The biologist shall possess a burrowing owl survey protocol training 
cerƟﬁcate issued by the Arizona Game and Fish Department. 
 Prior to construcƟon, all personnel who will be onsite, including, but not limited 
to, contractors, contractors’ employees, supervisors, inspectors, and 
subcontractors shall review the “Western Burrowing Owl Awareness” ﬂyer.  
 If any burrowing owls or acƟve burrows are idenƟﬁed, the contractor shall 
noƟfy the developer immediately.  No construcƟon acƟviƟes shall take place 
within 100 feet of any acƟve burrow.  If the developer, in cooperaƟon with the 
biologist, determines that burrowing owls cannot be avoided, the contractor 
shall employ a qualiﬁed biologist holding a permit from the U.S. Fish and 
Wildlife Service to relocate burrowing owls from the Property, as appropriate. 
 If vegetaƟon clearing occurs during the migratory bird breeding season (March 
1 – August 31), the contractor shall avoid any acƟve bird nests.  If the acƟve 
nests cannot be avoided, the contractor shall noƟfy the engineer for evaluaƟon.  
During the non-breeding season (September 1 – February 28), vegetaƟon 
removal is not subject to this restricƟon. 
Based on the completed Biological EvaluaƟon (provided as Appendix B), no potenƟal 
for “Take” exists (meaning that the Project has no potenƟal to harass, harm, pursue, 
hunt, shoot, wound, kill, trap, capture, or collect, or to aƩempt to engage in any such 
conduct) to threatened and endangered species. 
PotenƟal Waters of the United States (WOTUS) are not located on the Property.  
Therefore, SecƟon 404/401 permiƫng is not required or proposed for this 
development.

The Project will produce sanitary wastewater from bathrooms and other 
faciliƟes, as well as a small amount of industrial wastewater on an 
infrequent basis.  These wastewater streams will be blended together. 
The Project will discharge wastewater through a new 2-mile force main 
proposed to align with the LuckeƩ Road public ROW from the Property, 
southerly to a point of connecƟon to the exisƟng 27-inch gravity sewer at 
the intersecƟon with Sagebrush.  A new sewer liŌ staƟon is required to 
transfer the domesƟc wastewater uphill to the exisƟng line.  The ﬁnal 
locaƟon of the sewer liŌ staƟon will be conﬁrmed through the design 
process.

2. Water 
The Project will draw water from two sources.   
Potable water will be provided by a pipeline extension and upsizing a 
porƟon of exisƟng infrastructure.  The Project will connect to the Marana 
Water system via a 2.25-mile, 8-inch extension along LuckeƩ Road, Hardin 
Road and Wentz Road to the intersecƟon of Sagebrush Road.  The ﬁnal 
alignment will be determined by Town water modeling.  (See Exhibit 
III.F.2.a: Water Intent to Serve LeƩer and Exhibit III.F.2.b: Proposed Potable 
Water.) 
Industrial water and ﬁre water supply will be withdrawn from the adjacent 
CMID irrigaƟon canal.  CMID has indicated an average supply rate of 12 
cubic feet per second, or 7.8 million gallons (“MG”) per day, is available.  
CMID has conﬁrmed capacity is available to supply the non-potable water 
to the site for industrial purposes.  The Project’s esƟmated operaƟonal 
non-potable water needs average approximately 18 AFY , with a ﬁre storage 
requirement of up to 2 AFY .  The Applicant will also work with CMID on the 
landscaping and construcƟon water.  The current agricultural uses have an 
esƟmated demand of 950 to 1,250 AFY per year.   
The Project, at full build out, has an esƟmated average annual demand for 
both potable and non-potable water use of 40 AFY for all potenƟal uses.   
A water storage tank and booster pump are required to meet ﬁre ﬂow 
requirements for the Project.   
A leƩer from CMID aﬃrming suﬃcient water is available to serve the 
Project is provided as Appendix C.

A. Applicability 
The standards and guidelines contained with this Speciﬁc Plan shall supersede 
the standards within the MTC.  If an issue, situaƟon or condiƟon arises that is 
not addressed by this Speciﬁc Plan, the Speciﬁc Plan shall be administered and 
interpreted per SecƟon V.A of this Speciﬁc Plan. 
All construcƟon and development within the Speciﬁc Plan area shall comply 
with applicable provisions of various codes and regulaƟons adopted by the 
Town including, but not limited to, drainage, public water/wastewater 
infrastructure, building, ﬁre and lighƟng codes current at the Ɵme of 
development. 
 
B. Land Uses 
1. Primary Uses 
 Data Center 
 
2. Ancillary Uses 
 Land uses ancillary to the Data Center use or in support of employees of 
the Data Center use, including: 
o Retail (food, beverage, oﬃce supplies, pharmacy) 
o Coﬀee shop/bakery/café/restaurant  
o Mail/shipping service 
o RecreaƟon/gym/ﬁtness studio faciliƟes 
o Buildings and grounds maintenance (landscaping, faciliƟes or 
janitorial) 
o Recycling collecƟon  
o UƟlity switchyards and substaƟons with or without baƩery 
storage 
 BaƩery storage use is limited to the area designated for 
the switchyard/substaƟon use 
o Ancillary renewable energy systems (including solar, wind or 
waste-to-energy) 
o Water treatment/auxiliary water building/water storage 
o UƟlity operaƟons building 
o Central logisƟcs & security buildings 
o Parks, trails and funcƟonal open space areas 
 Uses that are not expressly permiƩed but are similar in type, scale and 
intensity to other ancillary uses allowed within the Speciﬁc Plan, and 
which are not otherwise unlawful, injurious to the general health or 
welfare, or speciﬁcally excluded, may be approved by the Zoning 
Administrator or their authorized designee. 
 
3. Prohibited Uses 
 Besides the Data Center use, no other Primary Uses are permiƩed within 
this Speciﬁc Plan, including, but not limited to, Mineral ExtracƟon.

D. Design Standards 
The following design standards apply to the Project. 
1. Noise Attenuation  
a. Baseline Sound Study 
An AcousƟc Report was prepared to determine baseline (ambient) noise 
levels surrounding the Property.  (See Appendix E.)   
Noise contour exhibits depicƟng the anƟcipated noise levels to be 
generated by the data center are included in the AcousƟc Modeling 
Results, provided as Appendix F. 
Except for site construcƟon work, the maximum sound levels for Project 
operaƟons adjacent to speciﬁc land uses will be as follows: 
ADJACENT USE MAXIMUM dBA DAYTIME/NIGHTTIME*

b. Post-Development Sound Study 
 A post-construcƟon noise study will be performed to document 
sound levels emanaƟng from the Project during peak operaƟon of 
the Project’s mechanical equipment measured at the Property line 
adjacent to residenƟal or other noise-sensiƟve land uses, as 
determined by the Zoning Administrator .  The post-construcƟon 
noise study must demonstrate that sound levels do not exceed the 
maximum sound levels set forth above.  The post-construcƟon 
noise study will occur prior to issuance of a cerƟﬁcate of 
occupancy or cerƟﬁcate of compleƟon, whichever occurs ﬁrst. 
 Annual sound study results will be provided to the Town within 30 
days of the anniversary of a cerƟﬁcate of occupancy being issued 
for the Project each year for a period of ﬁve years. 
 
2. Building Placement & Orientation 
 Buildings shall orient principal facades, including visitor, staﬀ and 
administraƟve entrances, to primary adjacent roads. 
 Loading docks and service entries of buildings shall not face or be 
visible from public ROW .  When possible, exisƟng buildings may be 
used to screen loading docks.  Where building locaƟons do not oﬀer 
screening, and in the case of phased development plans, screening of 
loading docks and service entries shall be accomplished by exisƟng or 
proposed landscaping, fencing, walls, or similarly eﬀecƟve methods. 
 Loading docks are permiƩed on only one side of the building. 
 Refuse collecƟon areas must be fully screened on all four sides.  
 
3. Generators 
 Generators and generator use must comply with all applicable state 
and federal regulaƟons. 
 If a data center uses diesel generators, only generators that meet Ɵer 
IV or newer emissions standards are permiƩed. 
 Except for generator tesƟng or commissioning acƟviƟes, generator 
use is limited to backup/emergency use only. 
Exhibit A to Marana Resolution 2026-005
SECTIO N IV: DEVELOPMENT & DESIGN REGULATIONS          LUCKETT ROAD NORTH SPECIFIC PLAN

 Generator tesƟng shall be limited to weekdays between 8:00 a.m. and 
5:00p.m., except in emergencies. 
 
4. Screening & Fencing 
 Use of fencing, including the use of wrought-iron, is permiƩed. 
 Chain-link fencing, with or without slaƩed inserts, and barbed wire 
fencing are prohibited along public or private street frontages. 
 SubstaƟons and mechanical equipment shall be screened from public 
and private ROW (except for private ROW that are internal to the 
Project) and from any adjacent property that is planned or zoned with 
any use other than industrial uses using one or mulƟple of the 
following methods of screening: 
o ExisƟng vegetaƟon that will remain on the property 
o Landscaping improvements constructed on the property 
o A visually solid fence, screen wall or panel, parapet wall, or 
other visually solid screen that shall be constructed of materials 
compaƟble with those used in the exterior construcƟon of the 
principal building; these features shall be at least ten feet in 
height; chain link fencing with slats is not permiƩed to saƟsfy 
this requirement 
o If the substaƟon is located visible to LuckeƩ Road, the screening 
shall be a 10-foot masonry wall located between LuckeƩ Road 
and the substaƟon in a locaƟon that does not interfere with site 
drainage.   
 Ground mounted mechanical equipment is prohibited in front yards. 
 Solid screening walls must be constructed with a design, materials, 
details, and treatment compaƟble with those used on the nearest 
principal facade of a building but may include perforated surfaces as 
needed for venƟlaƟon of mechanical equipment and/or facilitate site 
drainage. 
 Mechanical equipment shall be located and screened with materials 
that provide appropriate levels of noise aƩenuaƟon to reduce sound 
impacts on surrounding properƟes.  On properƟes adjacent to 
residenƟally zoned/planned properƟes, mechanical equipment must 
be screened on all four sides by an acousƟcal barrier.  For purposes of 
this secƟon, "acousƟcal barrier" is deﬁned as an exterior solid or 
louvred wall containing sound-prooﬁng materials designed to absorb 
noise and protect neighboring properƟes from noise polluƟon, or 
another eﬀecƟve miƟgaƟon measure speciﬁed by a qualiﬁed third-
party acousƟc engineer when found by the Zoning Administrator to 
provide appropriate levels of noise aƩenuaƟon.  AlternaƟvely, an 
acousƟcal barrier may be provided around the Project perimeter 
rather than surrounding each set of mechanical equipment. 
 Electric power lines, except electrical transmission lines carrying 48 
kV or more, shall be located underground. 
 
5. Landscape Buffers 
 Agricultural and commercial zoned/planned properƟes: A minimum 
50-foot buﬀer .  Buﬀering may be achieved through exisƟng mature 
landscaping or a landscaped earthen berm that screens the Property.  
Berms shall have a slope no steeper than 2:1 with a minimum height 
of four feet and planted with a minimum of 320 plant units per 100 
feet of ROW or Property line.   
 ResidenƟal or mixed-use zoned/planned properƟes: A minimum 100-
foot buﬀer.  Buﬀering may be achieved through exisƟng mature 
landscaping or a landscaped earthen berm that screens the Property.  
Berms shall have a slope no steeper than 2:1 with a minimum height

of six feet and planted and planted with a minimum of 320 plant units

6. Building Design 
a. Massing & Scale 
 Buildings shall use broad, large-scale architectural gestures to provide 
variety and modulaƟon in facade and massing as seen from public 
ROW .   
 VariaƟon at the ground plane shall be provided to create transiƟons in 
scale and mass as viewed from public ROW . 
 AddiƟve and subtracƟve shiŌs in the building footprint shall be 
provided to reduce mass and scale and to provide outdoor amenity 
spaces for employees and visitors. 
b. Principal Façade 
The following facade requirements apply to all principal façades, which 
are building façades that face adjacent exisƟng or planned public roads 
or that face property that is planned or zoned for any use other than 
industrial uses. 
 Principal facades must incorporate diﬀerenƟated surfaces at 
horizontal linear intervals that may vary in frequency, including: 
o FenestraƟon; and 
o A change in one of the following design elements: 
 Building material; 
 PaƩern; 
 Texture; 
 Color; or 
 Accent materials. 
 Principal facades must include building step-backs or recesses, which 
shall be a minimum of two feet in depth. 
 When a building has more than one principal facade, the principal 
façades must be consistent in terms of design, materials, details, and 
treatment. 
c. FenestraƟon 
 Each principal facade must include fenestraƟon comprising at least 
20% of the total surface coverage area of the principal facade. 
 The fenestraƟon must be compaƟble with the other design, materials, 
details, and treatment used on the same principal facade. 
d. Main Building Entryways 
 A data center building must include a main entrance feature that is 
diﬀerenƟated from the remainder of the building façade by a change 
inbuilding material, paƩern, texture, color, or accent material.  The 
entrance feature must also either project or recess from the adjoining 
building plane. 
e. Exterior Colors & Materials 
 Primary building facades shall use a neutral color paleƩe and avoid 
high-contrast colors. 
 Accent colors shall be selected to complement the dominant building 
color, and any color change shall occur where changes in the building 
plane or recesses are provided. 
 Colors shall not act as adverƟsements or billboards. 
 Building exteriors shall use materials with texture and character. 
 Changes in materials shall be reﬂected in massing or oﬀsets.  The 
number of disparate materials shall be limited to a maximum of three 
primary materials to avoid a busy appearance. 
 Design elements shall be used to enhance the overall expression of 
datacenter buildings, with an emphasis on the pedestrian experience, 
Exhibit A to Marana Resolution 2026-005
SECTIO N IV: DEVELOPMENT & DESIGN REGULATIONS          LUCKETT ROAD NORTH SPECIFIC PLAN 
 
 
 
 
46 
 
parƟcularly at entryways.  All buildings shall include at least ﬁve of the 
following architectural features: 
o Overhang 
o Canopy or porƟco 
o Recesses or projecƟons 
o Arcade 
o Raised corniced parapets over the entrance 
o Tower elements 
o VariaƟon in the roof line 
 
E. Parking 
Parking will be provided in accordance with MTC Chapter 17-9 (Parking). 
 
F. Signage 
Signage will be designed and permiƩed in accordance with MTC Chapter 17-10 
(Signs).  The Applicant will process a sign package at Ɵme of development. 
Address and unit number signage will be incorporated per Town Addressing 
requirements. 
 
G. Vegetation & Landscaping 
1. Native Plant Protection 
Per MTC § 17-11-5 (NaƟve Plant ProtecƟon).   
 
2. Landscape Requirements 
Per MTC § 17-11-8 (Landscape Standards for Oﬀ Street Parking Areas). 
Landscape buﬀers provided per Table IV.C and SubsecƟon IV.D.5, above. 
 
H. Lighting 
This Project is commiƩed to “Dark Skies” principles and the Town’s Outdoor 
LighƟng Code (“OLC”), which requires shielded lighƟng that does not spill over 
onto adjacent properƟes.  A lighƟng plan will be prepared and submiƩed with 
the Project’s building permit plans in accordance with the OLC’s permit 
submission requirements.

A. Administration & Interpretation 
The Speciﬁc Plan shall be administered by the Town’s Zoning Administrator, 
and all implementaƟon decisions shall be based on the purpose of the Speciﬁc 
Plan.  If a conﬂict arises between the Speciﬁc Plan and the MTC, the Speciﬁc 
Plan shall control.  If the Speciﬁc Plan is silent on any issue, and the MTC is 
consulted, the purpose and intent of the Speciﬁc Plan shall control the Zoning 
Administrator’s decision whether and how to apply the MTC.  Appeals of any 
Zoning Administrator interpretaƟon of this Speciﬁc Plan may be made to the 
Board of Adjustment within 15 days of the date of the interpretaƟon.  The 
Speciﬁc Plan will not result in the modiﬁcaƟon or change of any exisƟng Town-
adopted building codes. 
All subsequent land use and zoning enƟtlements, including plats and 
development plan submiƩals, shall be processed in accordance with 
established Town development review procedures. 
 
B. Implementation Responsibilities 
The Master Developer is the enƟty responsible for ensuring the basic 
infrastructure faciliƟes, including sewer, water and roads, are planned and 
constructed to serve the development areas within the Speciﬁc Plan in a 
Ɵmely and coordinated manner .   
Annual sound study results shall be provided to the Town within 30 days of the 
anniversary of a cerƟﬁcate of occupancy being issued for Project. 
C. Amendments 
Amendments to the Speciﬁc Plan are categorized as either administraƟve 
amendments or substanƟal amendments.   
The Zoning Administrator may approve administraƟve amendments, as 
deﬁned in MTC § 17-4-15(D), aŌer wriƩen applicaƟon is made.   
SubstanƟal amendments to the Speciﬁc Plan shall be those changes or 
modiﬁcaƟons that would result in a substanƟal change to the Speciﬁc Plan's 
regulaƟons or the overall intent of the Speciﬁc Plan, as deﬁned in MTC § 17-4-
15(D)(3).  SubstanƟal amendments to the Speciﬁc Plan shall be processed in 
accordance with MTC § 17-3-1. 
 
D. Phasing 
This Project will be developed in mulƟple phases over a ﬁve- to seven-year 
period.  Details of Project phasing will be determined at a later date and may 
be in conjuncƟon with the LuckeƩ Road South Speciﬁc Plan being processed 
simultaneously with this Speciﬁc Plan.

Jaguar North    1 
PROJECT LOCATION 
The Jaguar North project is located along Luckett Road, south of the Pinal Airpark Road , and west of  
Interstate 10 (I-10) in Marana, Pima County, Arizona (Figure 1. Vicinity Map). This Biological Evaluation 
(BE) has been prepared to support a Conditional Letter of Map Revision (CLOMR) application and the 303-
acre site consists of privately owned lands. Land use within t he site consists of agricultural land and 
associated improvements including agricultural canals and ditches and an agricultural pond . The 
surrounding land consists of agricultural land, a cemetery, a solar photovoltaic (PV) facility, a material 
source pit, and disturbed desert. 
 
The project is located in Sections 1-2, and 11-12 of Township 10 South, Range 10 East and Section 6 of 
Township 11 South, Range 11 East of the Gila and Salt River Base Line and Meridian and is depicted on 
the Red Rock, Arizona and West of Marana, Arizona 2021 USGS 7.5-minute topographic quadrangle maps 
(Figure 2. USGS 7.5-Minute Quadrangle Map).  
 
Throughout this BE, the construction footprint is defined as all area s of ground disturbance (303 acres). 
The action area includes all areas to be affected directly or indirectly by the action and not merely the 
construction footprint. In delineating the action area, we evaluated the farthest reaching physical, chemical, 
and biotic effects of the action on the environment , f ocusing on, but not exclusive to, the proposed 
construction of a campus style industrial development. 
PROJECT PURPOSE 
The purpose of this project is to construct a campus style industrial development (Jaguar North).  
SCOPE OF WORK 
The scope of work for this project includes:  
 
• Clear and grub vegetation onsite 
• Construct utilities 
• Construct roadway infrastructure 
• Construction onsite buildings

PROJECT CONSTRUCTION 
Construction phasing for the project consists of clearing and grubbing of vegetation onsite, mass grading 
and raising the site elevation with fill, installing utilities, and construction of roadway infrastructure and 
onsite buildings.  
Project Construction 
The contractor will clear/grub and mass grade the site to allow for stockpile and staging areas, installation 
of utilities, and construction of roadway infrastructure and onsite buildings. Clearing, grubbing, and grading 
are required to allow earth moving equipment such as an excavator, backhoe, bobcat, grader, bulldozer, 
front-end loader, or scraper to operate. A bulldozer , front-end loader, or excavator with claw attachment  
may be required to pick up or drag trees, shrubs, and groundcover to remove them from the construction 
footprint. If additional staging/stockpiling areas are required, they are the responsibility of the co ntractor. 
The next phase will consist of installation of utilities. Equipment such as a trencher, excavator, backhoe, or 
bobcat may be required to trench for pipe, conduit, or cables underground. After installation of utilities, 
roadway infrastructure would be constructed. Constructing new roadway would include grading and paving 
with asphalt and constructing curb and gutter with heavy machinery such as dump trucks, pickup trucks, 
front-end loaders or tractors , and a paving machine . After roadway infrastructure is constructed, onsite 
buildings would be constructed. Anticipated equipment would include cranes and heavy machinery to erect 
building walls, an excavator, backhoe, and bobcat. 
 
Construction Footprint  
Permanent impacts are defined as ground disturbance that would convert a pervious surface to an 
impervious surface. Due to the scope of work (mass grade), the entirety of the project limits (approximately 
303 acres) would be permanently impacted. 19 acres of vegetation, including trees, shrubs, and 
groundcover will be removed from the construction footprint during construction. The construction footprint 
is illustrated in Figure 3. Action Area Map. 
 
PROJECT ACTION AREA 
The action area includes all areas to be affected directly or indirectly by the action and not merely the 
construction footprint (50 CFR §402.02). In delineating the action area, we evaluated the farthest reaching 
physical, chemical, and biological effects of the action on the environ ment, focusing on, but not exclusive 
to the campus style industrial development. 
 
Potential effects of this action on the environment resulting from this project include loss of habitat due to 
removal of vegetation, potential degradation of suitable habitat due to the introduction of invasive plant 
species, sedimentation and associated water quality impacts from grubbing and grading within the 
agricultural pond, disruption of wildlife from construction noise. The action area was determined based on 
temporary construction noise which is the farthest-reaching impact.  
 
The removal of vegetation may cause loss of shelter and foraging habitat for insects, predator, and prey. 
Introduction of invasive plant species from construction equipment and activities can potentially allow for 
alteration or degradation of suitable habi tat of species. Introduction of these species could cause 
degradation of potentially suitable habitat for insects, predator, and prey. Approximately 19 acres of 
vegetation, including trees, shrubs, and groundcover will be removed during construction of thi s project. 
The removal of vegetation may cause loss of shelter and foraging habitat for wildlife. However, suitable 
habitat is available adjacent to the action area. 
 
Exhibit A to Marana Resolution 2026-005
 
Biological Evaluation 
Jaguar North    5 
Regarding sedimentation and associated water quality impacts from earth moving activities, construction 
will disturb more than one acre; therefore, a Section 402 (Arizona Pollutant Discharge Elimination System) 
permit and a Stormwater Pollution Prevention Plan (SWPPP) will be required from the Arizona Department 
of Environmental Quality (ADEQ).   
 
Sustained noise levels produced during construction can cause disruption of wildlife use of the action area. 
The ambient noise level within the construction footprint was estimated to be approximately 60 A-weighted 
decibels (dBA) (FHWA 2022). The action ar ea is based on the loudest piece of equipment used for the 
longest duration (a bulldozer) that will be utilized for approximately 18 months. A bulldozer, which produces 
a noise level of 85 dBA at 50 feet from the source on flat ground surface, was utilized as a point source to 
compare construction noise levels with the ambient noise produced by traffic on I -10 and the adjacent 
material source pit to evaluate potential noise impacts to wildlife. Leq is defined as the equivalent continuous 
noise level measured over a specific time period. In defining the action area for this project, the Roadway 
Construction Noise Model (RCNM) was used to determine the distance between the construction footprint 
and w here the construction noise would attenuate to ambient dBA m easured from L eq, which was 
determined to be approximately 400 feet (FHWA 2006). Construction noise could temporarily preclude 
wildlife from utilizing the action area; however, suitable habitat is available beyond the action area.  
 
The action area is defined as the construction footprint and 400 feet beyond in all directions ( Figure 3. 
Action Area Map).

LOCATION DESCRIPTION 
The project limits are approximately 1,900 feet above mean sea level (MSL) and slope/drain southwest. 
There are no Outstanding Arizona Waters (OAWs) or Impaired Streams in the project limits (ADEQ 2024).  
 
A site visit was conducted on December 19, 2024, by Kimley-Horn biologists (Avery Pritchard and Grace 
Harwin). Wildlife observed in the field included Cooper’s hawk (Accipiter cooperii), white-crowned sparrow 
(Zonotrichia leucophrys), Say’s phoebe ( Sayornis saya ), hooded oriole ( Icterus cucullatus ), and song 
sparrow (Melospiza melodia). Nests were not observed during field reconnaissance.  
 
According to Biotic Communities, Southwestern United States, the action area is within the Lower Colorado 
Subdivision of the Sonoran Desertscrub biotic community (Data Basin 2012). The action area is situated 
west of I-10, north of the Tucson Mountains, east of the Silver Bell Mountains, and west of the Tortolita 
Mountains. The terrain slopes west throughout out the project limits and is relatively flat. The predominant 
geological features along the action area includes Holocene  river alluvium (0 -10 ka) (USGS 2025). The 
following soils occur within the construction footprint: Gila loam, 0 to 1 percent slopes; Grabe silty clay loam; 
Anthony sandy lo am, 0 to 1 percent slopes and 1 to 3 percent slopes; and Denure sandy loam, 1 to 3 
percent slopes (USDA 2025). 
 
The action area consists of disturbed desert and agricultural land. Beyond the action area lands are 
primarily additional agricultural land, a solar PV facility , a cemetery, a material source pit,  and disturbed 
desert. 
 
An agricultural pond is the primary aquatic feature within the action area. The agricultural pond and irrigation 
ditches are manmade and used for agricultural irrigation. No  perennial or semi -perennial streams are 
present within the action area. See Figure 3. Action Area Map  for representative photographs of the 
construction footprint, action area, and surrounding landscape.  
 
Information from the Arizona Department of Water Resources (ADWR) was reviewed for water wells located 
within the vicinity of the action area. According to well registration records, groundwater exists at 
approximately 120 feet below ground surface (ADWR 2024). However, groundwater le vels can fluctuate 
due to seasonal variations, groundwater withdrawal or injection and other factors. 
 
The construction footprint is located within the action area and includes agricultural fields, an agricultural 
pond, and disturbed desert. The majority of the construction footprint consists of fallow agricultural fields. 
Dominant vegetation observed within the agricultural pond in the northeast corner of the site included velvet 
mesquite (Prosopis velutina), desertbroom (Baccharis sarothroides ), and Bermuda grass  (Cynodon 
dactylon). Within the northeast corner of the site, t he canopy primarily consists of velvet mesquite with a 
density of 90%. The subcanopy consists of desertbroom ranging from 10-30% density. Groundcover ranges 
from 0-90% with and consists of Bermuda grass and inland saltgrass (Disticlis spicata) near areas with  
surface water. Approximately 19 acres of vegetation, including trees, shrubs, and groundcover, will be 
removed during construction.

ENDANGERED SPECIES ACT (ESA) LISTED SPECIES  
SPECIES IDENTIFICATION 
Kimley-Horn obtained an official species list from the U.S. Fish and Wildlife Service (USFWS) Information 
for Planning and Consultation (IPaC) system on January 14, 2025. The list included eleven threatened, 
endangered, or candidate species that should be evaluated. A qualified biologist reviewed the  list to 
determine species that may occur in the project vicinity. Two species shown in Table 1 have the potential 
to occur in the project area and are analyzed in detail in Species Evaluation.

Life History 
Species Description and Ecology 
The yellow-billed cuckoo (YBCU) is a medium -sized bird with a slender, long -tailed profile and a stout, 
slightly down-curved bill that is blue-black above and yellow on the base of the lower mandible. Plumage is 
grayish brown above and white below, with re d primary flight feathers and tail feathers that are boldly 
patterned with black and white below. YBCUs have short, bluish gray legs, and adults have a narrow, yellow 
eye ring. As a member of the avian family Cuculidae, they share the common feature of a zygodactyl foot 
(two toes pointing forward and two toes pointing backward).  
 
The YBCU is a long-distance neotropical migrant and the western population breeds along river systems 
west of the Rocky Mountains, which separates them from the eastern yellow-billed cuckoo population. The 
western population is considered a biologically separate population segment; therefore, populations west 
of the Continental Divided have been identified as a Distinct Population Segment for conservation purposes. 
(Daw, 2014). 
 
YBCUs hunt while perched motionless on a tree branch, scanning foliage for prey items. During migration 
and breeding season, the species feeds primarily on insects such as caterpillars, cicadas, and katydids; 
but will also consume tree frogs and lizards. T he species migrates to Central and South America for the 
winter, where fruits and seeds are eaten more frequently (AGFD, 2011 and Daw, 2014). YBCUs have 
relatively large home ranges, with the average home range size covering over 100 acres; however, home 
ranges encompassing up to 500 acres have been recorded (USFWS, 2014b).  
 
Reproduction 
Typically, western YBCUs are late migraters and arrive on their breeding grounds in early to mid -June. 
Once on the breeding grounds, the species may time the onset of breeding to coincide with an abundance 
of large insects such as cicadas. Peak nesting season for the YBCU occurs from July to early August. Nests 
are placed on the horizontal branch of a tree such as a willow and both the male and female participate in 
constructing the nest. The nest is a loosely-built, flat, saucer-shaped, stick nest lined with bark and leaves. 
The cup nest is relatively shallow ranging from 2 to 4 centimeters deep and is usually positioned 1 to 6 
meters above ground; below a dense patch of canopy and concealed by surrounding foliage. YBCUs will 
Exhibit A to Marana Resolution 2026-005
 
Biological Evaluation 
Jaguar North    13 
lay 3 to 4 unmarked, pale greenish-blue eggs. Incubation lasts 9 to 11 days and eggs will change color to 
greenish-yellow before hatching. Young are born altricial and will leave the nest 6 to 9 days after hatching. 
On occasion the species may parasitize broods of other cuckoos (AGFD, 2011 and Daw, 2014).  
 
Suitable Habitat  
The western population of YBCUs occupy riparian woodlands along perennial rivers and streams at 
elevations below 6,600 feet. Cottonwood -willow galleries are most often inhabited; however, salt cedar, 
mesquite bosques, and other riparian tree communities may also be used. The species requires relatively 
large contiguous patches of multi -layered riparian habitat with dense understory foliage for nesting. The 
multi-layered canopy structure provides the necessary microclimate of cooler temperatures and higher 
humidity needed for successful nesting (AGFD, 2011 and Daw, 2014). 
 
PBFs for YBCU habitat were identified as three distinct habitat elements; riparian woodland structure, 
availability of prey populations, and riverine processes. The PBFs that were identified in the April 2021 Final 
Rule Designation of Critical Habitat for the species include: 
 
PBF 1 —Riparian woodlands; mesquite woodlands (mesquite -thorn-forest), and Madrean evergreen 
woodland drainages. 
 
a. Rangewide breeding habitat (including areas in the Southwest)  – woodlands within 
floodplains or in upland areas or terraces often greater than 325 feet in width and 200 
acres or more in extent with an overstory and understory vegetation component in 
contiguous or nearly contiguous patches adjacent to intermittent or  perennial 
watercourses.  The slope of the watercourses is generally less than 3 percent but may 
be greater in some instances.  Nesting sites within the habitat have an above average 
canopy closure (greater than 70 percent), and have a cooler, more humid environment 
than the surrounding riparian and upland habitats. 
 
b. Southwestern breeding habitat – composed of more arid riparian woodlands (including 
mesquite bosques), desert scrub and desert grassland drainages with a tree 
component, and Madrean evergreen woodlands (oak and other tree species), in 
perennial, intermittent, and ephemeral drainages.  These more arid riparian woodland 
drainages also bisect other habitat types, including Madrean evergreen woodland, 
native and nonnative desert grassland, and desert scrub.  More than one habitat type 
within and adjacent to the drainage may contribute towa rd nesting habitat. 
Southwestern breeding habitat is more water-limited, contains a greater proportion of 
xero-riparian and non -riparian plant species, and is often narrower, more open, 
patchier, or sparser than elsewhere in the DPS and may persist only as narrow bands 
or scattered patches along the bankline or as small in -channel islands.  The habitat 
contains a tree or large -shrub component with a variable overstory canopy and 
understory component that is sometimes less than 200 acres.  Riparian trees (including 
xero-riparian) in these ecosystems may even be more sparsely distributed and less 
prevalent than non -riparian trees.  Adjacent habitat may include managed (mowed) 
nonnative vegetation or terraces of mesquite or other drought -tolerant species within 
the floodplain.  In narrow or arid ephemeral drainages, breeding habitat commonly 
contains a mix of non-riparian vegetation found in the base habitat as well as riparian 
(including xero-riparian) trees.
